In August 1932 Rev. and Mrs. van der Smissen left Berne for Newton, Kansas, to live in the Bethel Home for Aged. It was there that Mrs. van der Smissen passed away in 1934, on 

May 24, the birthday anniversaries of her son Alwin, and her son-in-law, Mr. Voran, husband of her daughter Hilda. 

SAMUEL LEHMAN 

Little did Sam Lehman realize back in the nineties when he began writing a chronology of Mennonite families and recording important memoranda that his book would be one of the greatest and best sources of information for this history. 

We say back in the nineties because it was in 1898 that 

S. F. Sprunger's record stopped. So it seems fair to assume that it was because Mr.

Lehman had begun his records that Rev. Sprunger quit keeping his own. 

If it were not for Mr. Lehman we would not have the marriage records prior to 1871, or yearly baptismal records, except S. F. Sprunger's, prior to 1914 when "Year" books became a yearly feature or any data concerning the election and ordination of ministers, or a list of early Mennonite families, or other valuable data. 

Samuel Lehman

So we wish to pay tribute to the man who spent years in collecting this valuable information. On rainy days, the family knew exactly where to find him. He sat at his desk for hours at a time and no one will ever be able to compute the number of hours spent at this priceless task of tracing the Mennonite

families from their ancestry on the Sonnenberg and Münsterberg in the Jura mountains of Switzerland, to their descendants here, up to the time of his death. He even made a trip to Switzerland in 1906 to perfect and correct his data. His book is a very valuable one.

We do not know for certain just when Mr. Lehman first became interested in a record of this kind. Perhaps it dates back to the time when he became undertaker. Christian B. 

Lehman, as we have read elsewhere, was the first undertaker. 

When Christian Stauffer died January 20, 1892, Mr. Lehman became ill and could not finish the coffin. So he called his neighbor, Sam Lehman, over to help him. When Christian 

Lehman passed away a few days later, on January 25, Sam Lehman made his coffin, the first he made alone. Thus he became successor to Christian B. Lehman. 

Mr. Lehman had many interesting experiences as undertaker. When he purchased a hearse, sometime between 1892 and 1895, there were some who did not approve of it and when they had need of a funeral conveyance gave orders that the old spring wagon should be used. His interest in the appearance of things later led him to buy two beautiful black horses, a perfect match, and shiny new harness to complete the outfit.

When he began buying coffins many thought them too fancy, so he had to continue making them over a long period of years. There were some who did not approve of the shiny handles, especially for children, so people usually carried the small ones on their shoulders. Before "der alt Mann," Abraham Sprunger, died, he gave orders not to have any handles put on his coffin. They had to use strips of heavy cloth and slip them under the coffin and carry it that way. But this was before Mr. Lehman's day as undertaker. 

Many church members remember many early instances where it would have been better to have had the bodies embalmed. Mr. Lehman also realized this need, so paid someone to go away and learn the business.

People sometimes have to change their ideas along certain

BIOGRAPHIES 

245 lines whether they want to or not. Surely the fast automobiles of today are a decided change from the early spring wagons and carriages. For a long time people thought a funeral procession must be slow. The horses had to walk all the way, no trotting being allowed. Mr. Lehman often had orders beforehand to be sure and keep his horses at a walk. This dragged out the funerals considerably, so it often was after dark when the bereaved families returned home. S. F.

Sprunger did not approve of the slow processions. We often drove with him on such occasions and we remember well how he, being at the head of the procession, began hurrying them along.

Sometimes the roads were so bad that four horses had to be hitched to the hearse.

Mr. Lehman enjoyed the work as undertaker and continued in it until Eli Bierie came to Berne and started a competitive business. After Henry Schindler had learned the work of embalming, the two continued the business together until about 1908. 

Mr. Lehman was of a quiet, unassuming nature, not one to go about bragging that he had done this or that. He believed in and carried out the injunction "let not thy left hand know what thy right hand doeth."

His reputation as an able carpenter was well established. 

When the church was built in 1879 he worked hard and long, often working even after night. He again was a willing and valuable worker when the church was enlarged in 1886 and again in 1899. When it was altered in 1899 it was Mr. Lehman who built the steeple for the bell. When the 1912 church was built he served as superintendent and throughout the entire building period worked very hard at his task. It has been thought that he worked too hard for his health was somewhat undermined after that. 

Mr. Lehman was born June 7, 1841 on Sonnenberg, Wayne 

County, Ohio, the son of Christian and Magdalena Geiger

Lehman. He grew to young manhood in that historic old 

Swiss Settlement where he was married November 30, 1865 to

Elizabeth, daughter of Peter and Susanna Kennel Oberly.

Mrs. Lehman passed away in September 1866 shortly after her daughter Josephine was born. The little baby followed her in death shortly after.

On January 16, 1867, Mr. Lehman came to Adams County, Indiana. The following October he married Elizabeth, daughter of Abraham B. and Elizabeth Zuercher Sprunger. The following spring they settled on their big farm just northwest of town which he farmed until December 1903 when they moved into a new brick house in the south part of town. 

His quarter section of land was known far and wide as a model farm, for he took great pride in the best possible improvements that made for the most successful operation. 

But Mr. Lehman did not confine his interests to farming, carpentry and undertaking. Almost from the beginning of 

Berne's history, he became one of the most substantial promoters of business interests here. With his brothers-in-law, A. A. Sprunger and John A. Sprunger, he was a member of the firm of Sprunger, Lehman and Company from its beginning to the time of his death and helped to build the first brick block in Berne, the Champion Block. He was a heavy investor in many important business enterprises in Berne. 

Mr. Lehman had two brothers, John and Christian. The latter two were very conservative. When the former wanted them to have their picture taken with him, they were very reluctant to do so, but they finally consented. 

By the second marriage Mr. Lehman had eleven children. 

One son, Daniel, died in infancy, but six sons and four daughters all grew to manhood and womanhood and are still living. 

They are: Mrs. Isaac Lehman of Berne, William of Tracy, California, Mrs. John Zweiacher of Geary, Oklahoma, Solomon 

A. of Fort Wayne, Indiana, Mrs. Amos Burkhalter of Berne, Noah M. of Berne, Enos W. of Berne, Leonard F. of Fort Wayne, Indiana, Mrs. William Hirschy of Phoenix, Arizona, and Wilbur 

H. of Columbus, Ohio.

Mr. Lehman passed away June 10, 1914, Mrs. Lehman on 

January 25, 1932.

CHURCH MEMBERS IN CHRISTIAN SERVICE 

And they that be wise shall shine as the brightness of the firmament and they that turn many to righteousness as the stars forever and ever. Dan. 12:3.

Blessed are the dead which die in the Lord from henceforth: Yea, saith the Spirit, that they may rest from their labours; and their works do follow them. 

Rev. 14:13. "No service in itself is small, None great, though earth it fill:

But that is small that seeks its own, And great, that seeks God's will." -Author unknown.

Recognition is here given to the work of the following men and women, who, while members of the Mennonite Church of Berne, Indiana, have gone out and given or are giving full time service to Christian work, some for shorter and some for longer periods. 

Christian Sprunger 

Samuel F. Sprunger

Isaac A. Sommer Joel Sprunger Joel Lehman Noah C. Hirschy Martha Sprunger Welty 

James Sprunger 

Matilda Lehman Hirschy

Menno M. Lehman 

Elmer J. Neuenschwander 

Caroline Burkhalter Zook 

Frieda Sprunger Boehr 

Amalia Lehman

Marvin Lehman

Christian Sprunger (see biographical section).

Samuel F. Sprunger (see biographical section). 

Isaac A. Sommer (see biographical section). 

Joel Sprunger left Berne in 1886, and after studying in Kansas for one year went to the Darlington, Oklahoma, mission station, where for one and one-half years he served as teacher in the industrial mission boarding school which was then being operated in connection with the Mennonite mission work among the Indians. 

He was ordained to the ministry by Rev. H. R. Voth May 

14, 1893, and served the Mennoville, Oklahoma, Mennonite 

Church for twenty-seven years.

Joel Lehman served as evangelist and as an assistant to Rev. S. F. Sprunger from 1888 to 1890. In 1890 he received a call from the Salem Mennonite Church in Wayne County,

Ohio. There, on March 27, 1891, he was ordained as elder by 

Ephraim Hunsberger, and served that church for twelve years. 

In 1902 Mr. Lehman returned to Berne, and the following year substituted for S. F. Sprunger during his illness. Since then he has substituted on various occasions in his home church. 

Noah C. Hirschy was ordained into the ministry by Rev. 

S. F. Sprunger on Sunday, August 7, 1892. He received a call from the Wadsworth Mennonite Church, preaching part time. while attending Oberlin College and Seminary and on May 6, 1894 was ordained as elder by the Rev. Ephraim Hunsberger to the full ministry of that same church. He also served the 

Canton, Ohio, congregation during this period; and for a few years conducted an academy and normal school in the old 

Mennonite College building at Wadsworth. 

In the fall of 1900 he accepted the call to the presidency of Central Mennonite College at Bluffton, Ohio. He continued there until 1909, when he accepted the call to become president of Redfield College, South Dakota (Congregational), where he labored five years, after which for health considera-

tions he spent five years in the South, Texas and Louisiana. 

There, in connection with fruit culture, he taught, preached, and lectured under the auspices of the Congregational Board of Missions. 

In 1920 he accepted a position in Berea College, Kentucky, where he taught until his death in 1925. 

One year, during the period of labor at Bluffton College, was spent in Europe for study and travel. Wherever he was engaged in school work he also did considerable preaching. 

Martha Sprunger (Mrs. P. P. Welty) spent the years from 

1899 to 1902 at Cantonment, Oklahoma, as a helper in the 

Mennonite Indian Mission. 

Mrs. Welty considers those three years full of rich experiences and blessing, overseeing the cooking and breadmaking, and being responsible for the feeding of the many Indian children in the boarding school, as well as the missionary workers. Thus she was brought in contact with real missionary work.

James Sprunger preached at the Wadsworth and Sterling Mennonite Churches from 1901 to 1904 while attending the Oberlin Seminary. He also worked for the Mennonite Publishing Company and Book Concern while attending Oberlin College, during his summer vacations. Two summers he spent in Canada among the Mennonites of Manitoba, Saskatchewan and Alberta. It was at that time that he received a permit to enter the otherwise closed settlement of the Dukhobors. 

In 1904 he did City Mission work at Niagara Falls, New York. During part of his time at Oberlin he also served as secretary of the college Y. M. C. A.

In 1905 he was taken to California after a very severe operation, from which he never fully recovered. In the fall of that year he recovered sufficiently to take up the work as secretary of the Y. M. C. A. at Long Beach, California. In 1908 he became Field Secretary of the State Y. M. C. A. of California, and in 1910 State Secretary, which position he held as long as he lived.

In 1915, during the World War, he was sent to Europe by the National Y. M. C. A. to work among the prison camps, where he worked for eight months, when he had to return home, because of ill health. After his return home he again resumed his work as far as his health permitted him. 

Mr. Sprunger passed away August 10, 1918 at the age of forty-seven years. 

Matilda Lehman (Mrs. William Hirschy) served in the

Chicago Hebrew Mission from 1909 to 1913. 

Menno M. Lehman's first field of labor was in Drake, Saskatchewan, from June 1912 to September 1915, serving under the appointment of the Home Mission Board of the General Conference. He was ordained as an evangelist by 

Rev. N. F. Toews, in 1912, at Drake, Saskatchewan. He did considerable supply preaching while attending Bluffton College. In 1919 he received a call from the Mennonite Church at Wadsworth, Ohio, and after being ordained to the office of elder by Rev. P. R. Schroeder he served the Wadsworth church from 1919 to 1922. 

He was superintendent of the Chicago Mennonite Mission from 1923 to 1927. From 1927 to 1936 he again served under the Home Mission Board at Drake, Saskatchewan. At present he is serving the church at Monroe, Washington, and has done so since February 1, 1937.

Elmer J. Neuenschwander was ordained to the ministry in July 1914 by Rev. S. F. Sprunger, and has served the following churches as pastor and elder: First Mennonite Church, Aberdeen, Idaho, from August 1914 to August 1920; Salem 

Church, Freeman, South Dakota, August 1920 to 1924; Ebenezer 

Church, Bluffton, Ohio, September 1924 to 1929; Hoffnungsfeld Church, McPherson, Kansas, November 1929 to 1934; 

Bethany Church, Kingman County, Kansas, June 1935 to the present date.

Beside his pastoral work he has done considerable evangelistic work, having conducted over twenty special series of revival meetings.

Mrs.

Caroline Burkhalter Zook did missionary work at the 

Moen Copi Mission, Hopi Indian Reservation, Tuba City, Arizona, from August 1914 to September 1922. She was ordained by Rev. M. M. Horsch assisted by Rev. J. B. Frey, at 

Upland, California.

Frieda Sprunger (Mrs. P. J. Boehr) entered the deaconess work in 1914 and was ordained as such by Rev. H. D. Penner, and served as a deaconess from 1914 to 1920, part of the time in Newton, Kansas, and a part of the time in Berne, Indiana.

In the fall of 1921 she went to China after having been ordained as a missionary by Rev. S. M. Musselman. She has worked at the following stations: Tamingfu, Kai Chow, Nanlo, and Chang Yuan. Her work has been both evangelistic and medical (nursing).

Sister Amalia Lehman began the deaconess work in 1915, when she entered the Bethel Deaconess Training School. She was ordained as a deaconess June 1, 1919, by Rev. H. D.

Penner. The ordination sermon was preached by Rev. J. W. 

Kliewer. She has served at the following hospitals: Bethany 

Deaconess Hospital, American Falls, Idaho, October 1919 to

April 1921; Bethel Deaconess Hospital, Mountain Lake, Minne sota, October 1923 to August 1929; the remainder of the time she has worked at the Bethel Deaconess Hospital, Newton, Kansas, which is her permanent address. 

Sister Amalia writes, "Besides the daily nursing, there is great satisfaction in having devotion with the patients, and in distributing the gospel tracts to those who sojourn in our institution. 'Little is much when God is in it.' 9 99 Rev. Andrew J. Neuenschwander was ordained as a minister and elder by Dr. J. H. Langenwalter, assisted by Rev. 

A. S. Shelly, on September 22, 1918. He served as supply pastor under the evangelization committee of the Middle District Conference from October 1, 1916 to November 25, 1917.

He has served the following churches as pastor and elder: 

Apostolic Mennonite Church, Trenton, Ohio, January 1918 to

January 1921; First Mennonite Church, Philadelphia, Penn sylvania, February 1921 to August 1929; First Mennonite 

Church, Upland, California, September 1929 to August 1933; 

Swamp Charge, Quakertown, Pennsylvania, October 1934 to the present date. 

He served as Field Secretary for the General Conference

Young People's Committee and also for the Publication Board, September 1934 to August 1935. 

Martha Burkhalter sailed for India as a missionary in the fall of 1917, and has labored there ever since, excepting when she was home on furlough. Her work in India has been evangelistic and educational. Her educational work has been in connection with the Funk Memorial School and the Bible School in Janjgir, India. One year, while on furlough, she served on the faculty at Bluffton College. 

Rev. Alfred and Barbara Hirschy Habegger have been active in missionary work among the Cheyennes on the Tongue River, Indian Reservation, for the last twenty years, preaching and teaching God's word. They were both ordained as missionaries on May 19, 1918, by Rev. J. W. Kliewer. 

Christine Habegger (Mrs. M. H. Leiper) went to China in the fall of 1918 and served as a missionary under the Mennonite General Conference Board for four and one-half years. 

Her ordination took place in August 1918 by Rev. P. R. Schroeder.

Metta Lehman (Mrs. David Dean) was ordained as a missionary in August 1918 by Rev. P. R. Schroeder. She served in China for eight years-four years under the Mennonite

General Conference Board, and for four years she did industrial work which was interdenominational. 

Noah Burkhalter, with his wife, formerly Adah Good, sailed for India on July 23, 1919. Their ordination as missionaries took place June 29, 1919 by Rev. S. M. Musselman, assisted by Rev. Paul Whitmer. Mr. Burkhalter's time of service in India was very short-only six months. The Lord called him to his eternal home April 12, 1920, after a siege

of typhoid fever. Why his service in India was so short we do not understand, but one thing we know—God doeth all things well.

Martha Franz (Mrs. J. L. Westrich) was ordained as a home missionary by Rev. W. S. Gottshall on August 9, 1919.

Soon after she entered work at the Altoona Mission, Altoona, Pennsylvania, and served as a city missionary, doing house visitation, conducting meetings, working with the children and doing mission work in the slums of Altoona, until September 1922. She then went to New York City, where she worked at Mariner's Temple, in the heart of Chinatown and among the drunkards of the Bowery. In June 1924 she received a call from the New York City Mission Society in

New York to become their city missionary. She accepted this call and served till July 1, 1926, when she married J. L. Westrich. 

Grover Soldner was ordained to the eldership, April 18, 1920, in the First Mennonite Church, Bluffton, Ohio, at the request of the Mennonite Church of Summerfield, Illinois, to which church he was called as pastor. The ordination sermon was preached by Rev. P. E. Whitmer, and the ordination service was read by Rev. A. S. Shelly, then pastor of the First Mennonite Church. Dr. S. K. Mosiman also assisted in the "laying on of hands."

Prior to his ordination he served as supply pastor for the 

Mennonite Church, Sterling, Ohio, for nine months from the summer of 1919 to the spring of 1920. Later he served the following churches: Mennonite Church, Summerfield, Illinois, July 1, 1920 to June 24, 1923; Zion Mennonite Church, Souderton, Pennsylvania, July 15, 1923 to May 31, 1931; Eighth 

Street Mennonite Church, Goshen, Indiana, June 21, 1931 to 

May 23, 1937.

He was named Field Secretary of Bluffton College in 

September 1936, serving in part time capacity until June 1, 1937, and in full time capacity since that date.

He served as a member of the General Conference Board of Foreign Missions from 1926 to 1935.

Mrs. Metta Habegger Moyer, with her husband, S. T. 

Moyer, arrived in India on Thanksgiving Day in 1920, where they have served as missionaries since. They spent the first two years in Janjgir, C. P. India, and the remainder of the time in Basna.

Mrs. Moyer has been engaged chiefly in evangelistic work, both at the mission stations and in the districts. Her work among the Christians has been through women's Bible classes, sewing classes, Sunday School work, and Christian home and child training. In the districts the work is teaching inquirers, supervising gospel teams and women Bible teachers. 

Rev. and Mrs. Moyer were ordained as foreign missionaries at Souderton, Pennsylvania in October 1920, by Rev. S. 

M. Musselman. 

Levina Burkhalter left Berne on September 20, 1920, for Los Angeles, after receiving a call from the Home Mission Board of the General Conference, to take up work as a city missionary in the Whosoever Will Mission of that city. Realizing her need for more Bible training, she asked permission from the board to attend the Los Angeles Bible Institute from September to April of that year, which was granted. In April 

1921 she entered into full time service at the above mentioned mission, and has been in the same kind of work to this date. 

However, in the meantime, the Whosoever Will Mission has developed into an established church and is now called the 

Immanuel Mennonite Church of Los Angeles. In December 

1935 her work under the Home Mission ended, but the church voted to have her continue in the work as before, serving as their church visitor. 

She was ordained on May 29th, 1921, by Rev. P. W. Penner, who at that time was pastor of the Mission where she started her service. 

Loretta Lehman (Mrs. Robert Blackwood) left for Champa,

C. P. India, as a missionary, April 19, 1921, and served as such till November 1937. Her work was primarily evangelistic, but being a trained nurse, she contributed much towards the medical side of missionary work, by working in the hospital

and caring for the sick. She was ordained as a missionary 

April 17, 1921, by Rev. S. M. Musselman.

Howard Liechty was in full time Christian service from September 1926 until June 1930 and from September 1932 until June 1935, a total of about seven years. He worked in Gerard 

Institute, a school for boys, Sidon, Syria, under the American 

Presbyterian Mission of Syria. 

In giving us the above information, Howard adds, "Those seven years of Christian service were the happiest I have ever experienced, and I owe it all to my early training in a Christian home and in the church at Berne, through its ministers and Sunday School teachers, and Christian Endeavor, and to my years of college in Bluffton."

Hilda Reusser Dester is serving as a missionary in Basna, C. P. India. Dr. and Mrs. Herbert Dester went to India in 

October 1927. Mrs. Dester is teaching in and supervising the boarding school at Basna, besides helping her husband in the medical work. On October 9, 1927, she was ordained as a missionary by Rev. P. R. Schroeder.

Sylvan Lehman has been engaged in Home Mission work since November 9th, 1929. He was ordained to the ministry

November 3, 1929, by Rev. J. E. Amstutz, a member of the 

Home Mission Board, and to the office of full eldership by Rev. 

W. S. Gottshall on May 1, 1932.

His first field of labor was at the Smith Corner Mennonite Church, East Freedom, Pennsylvania, where he worked from November 1929 to April 1935, and since April 1935, he is serving the Calvary Mennonite Church at Mechanics Grove, Pennsylvania. 

Helen Nickel went to Janjgir, C. P. India, on November 

17, 1929, and after learning the language took supervision of the Anna Funk Memorial Boarding School, which position she has held ever since except when home on furlough. Her ordination took place at Berne, Indiana, and was conducted by Dr. J. W. Kliewer, assisted by Rev. P. R. Schroeder and 

Rev. C. H. Suckau.

John Nussbaum was ordained as an elder in Berne, Indiana, by Rev. C. H. Suckau, assisted by Rev. A. J. Neuenschwander and Rev. Joel Lehman, on January 29, 1933. His work, which has been evangelistic almost exclusively, has been performed in Indiana, Michigan, Ohio, Pennsylvania, Alabama, and Canada.

At present he is serving a Christian Missionary Alliance 

Church in Cleveland, Ohio.

Edison Habegger entered the Lord's work eight years ago and has served as an evangelist in the United States, Canada, England, Ireland and Scotland. He was ordained to the ministry in Berne, Indiana, by Rev. C. H. Suckau on January 29, 1933.

Marvin Lehman served as a Home Missionary in Breathitt 

County, Kentucky, from June 1932 to September 1933. His work was evangelistic, preaching and supervising Sunday Schools. He, with Glenna Duff Lehman, were ordained on 

January 29, 1933, by Rev. C. H. Suckau, when she joined him in his work.

OLD PEOPLE IN THE CHURCH

CONFERENCE, GENERAL AND DISTRICT 

S. F. Sprunger's two congregations joined the General

Conference in 1872, sessions being held in Wadsworth, Ohio, October 28. S. F. Sprunger represented the church with one vote.

The Berne Church entertained the following sessions of the General Conference: 

The tenth session, November 6-12, 1884, six votes. 

Delegates were S. F. Sprunger, Jacob Lehman, Peter 

Bixel and Frederick Sprunger. 

Fraternal delegates from the Christian Sprunger congregation were Rev. Christian Sprunger and Christian 

B. Lehman. 

The sixteenth session, October 15-21, 1902, twenty votes.

The delegates were: S. F. Sprunger, I. A. Sommer, Joel Lehman, J. F. Lehman, C. A. Neuenschwander and Frederick Sprunger. 

The twenty-fourth session, August 22-29, 1926, thirty-three votes. 

The delegates were: P. R. Schroeder, C. H. A. van der 

Smissen, Joel Lehman, J. F. Lehman, J. P. Habegger, J. F. Sprunger, Jerry Liechty, Benjamin Sprunger, D. 

C. Sprunger, D. J. Sprunger, C. A. Neuenschwander and Elmer W. Baumgartner. 

S. F. Sprunger's congregations joined the Western District Conference about 1876, and entertained the Conference in the Baumgartner church in French Township November 

18, 1878. Later, Berne was one of the charter members of the Middle District Conference when it was organized in 1888. Berne

MEMORANDA 

267 entertained the Middle District Conference in the following years: 

1891, October 1, 2, fourteen votes. Delegates were: 

Rev. Christian Sprunger, S. F. Sprunger, J. A. Sprunger, I. A. Sommer, Frederick Sprunger, D. Z. Sprunger, D. C. Sprunger, A. A. Sprunger, C. W. Baumgartner,

C. A. Neuenschwander, C. A. Augsburger, J. F. Leh man, John Amstutz and Joel Welty.

Chairman, S. F. Sprunger. Secretary, C. H. A. van der 

Smissen. 

1899, October 4-9, eighteen votes. Delegates were:

S. F. Sprunger, I. A. Sommer, J. F. Lehman, C. A. 

Neuenschwander, C. W. Baumgartner and Frederick Sprunger.

Chairman, N. C. Hirschy. Secretary, I. A. Sommer. 

1909, August 30, 31, twenty-seven votes. Delegates were: 

J. W. Kliewer, S. F. Sprunger, Joel Lehman, I. A. 

Sommer, C. W. Baumgartner, C. A. Neuenschwander and Frederick Sprunger. 

Chairman, S. K. Mosiman. Secretary, C. H. A. van der

Smissen. 

1920, August 25, 26, thirty-three votes. Delegates were: 

P. R. Schroeder, C. H. A. van der Smissen, Ben 

Sprunger, J. F. Sprunger, D. J. Sprunger, D. C. 

Sprunger, J. F. Lehman and C. A. Neuenschwander.

Chairman, A. S. Shelley. Secretary P. P. Hilty. 

1932, August 17-21, thirty-eight votes. Delegates were: 

C. H. Suckau, E. A. Luginbill, Ben Sprunger, D. J. 

Sprunger, and Jerry Liechty. 

Chairman, W. W. Miller. Secretary, E. W. Baumgartner.

In 1938 when the Conference was held in Trenton, Ohio, the Berne Church had forty-three votes.

BAPTISM 

There are no records of baptism for the early years, but there is a record that tells when some of the early members were baptized. We cannot say for certain how many were baptized by the various pastors, but we have tried to check carefully, and we believe the following record to be nearly correct: 

Christian Baumgartner

Peter S. Lehman 

Ulrich Kipfer 20 5 After 1869 we have the following records: 

Christian Sprunger 

S. F. Sprunger 

J. W. Kliewer 

P. R. Schroeder

C. H. Suckau 

65 638 271 536 410 The largest number baptized at any given time: 

89, Sunday, February 9, 1902 

66, Sunday, July 25, 1915 

59, Good Friday, March 23, 1894 

57, Good Friday, April 8, 1898

Christian Sprunger's first class was in 1869, on Good Friday, March 26. The following four were in the class: 

Anna Lehman 

Christina Luginbill (John's daughter) 

Katherine Luginbill 

Jeremias Luginbill

S. F. Sprunger baptized Elisabeth Riff and Abraham C. 

Neuenschwander in October, 1874, but on different occasions. 

His first class was on October 24, 1875, departing from the usual custom of baptizing on Good Friday. The following seven persons were in the class: 

Maria Beer 

Elisabeth Liechty

The class of 1886 was a large one and followed in the wake of a great revival. The class was baptized on the Sunday the church was re-dedicated, on September 12. There were fiftyone in the class, twenty-five young women and twenty-six young men.

MARRIAGES 

From August 22, 1844 to June 5, 1938, five hundred fortyseven marriages for church members were performed by the following pastors: 

David Baumgartner 4 

Christian Baumgartner 

Ulrich Kipfer 10 

Peter S. Lehman 13 

Christian Sprunger 23 

S. F. Sprunger 172 

Joel Lehman 

J. W. Kliewer 51 

P. R. Schroeder 149 

C. H. A. van der Smissen 3 

C. H. Suckau 106 

The greatest number in any given year were: 

19 by P. R. Schroeder in 1920 

16 by C. H. Suckau in 1934 

14 by S. F. Sprunger in 1913

No dedicatory services were held for the first church, and as far as we know there were no church weddings before 

October 31, 1867. On that day there was a double wedding.

According to the records the first marriages after each church dedication were performed as follows:

BIRTHS 

Yearly birth records were not kept until 1918, but we list the first that occurred after each church dedication. 

Caroline, daughter of Benjamin and Elisabeth Riff Nussbaum, born December 20, 1879. (Now Mrs. Simon Lehman). 

Hilda, daughter of Jephtha and Katherine Nussbaum 

Sprunger, born September 20, 1886. 

Luella, daughter of George and Anna Bixler McCune, born August 2, 1899.

Arlo, son of Oswin and Emma Schmidt Lehman, born 

April 23, 1912.

Sherman, son of Walter and Ada Lehman Stucky, born 

April 23, 1912.

MEMORANDA 

297 

FUNERALS 

In 1893 Rev. S. F. Sprunger made the following statement in the first year book published: "In the past twenty years I have conducted one hundred eighteen funerals, ninety of which were for church members."

There are no records for funerals prior to October, 1911, but it may interest some to know whose funerals were the first to be held after each church dedication. 

The first after November 9, 1879; Ana Lisa, daughter of 

Peter A. and Katherine Sprunger, died November 11, 1879, aged two years. 

The first after September 12, 1886: Peter Luginbill, died 

October 18, 1886, aged sixty-eight years. 

The first after July 30, 1899: Elroy, son of Gottfried and 

Maria Welty Burry, died August 14, 1899, aged four months. 

The first after April 7, 1912: Caesar Brandt child, funeral on April 18, 1912.

The largest mass funeral was held June 1, 1931, for the five members of the Charles O. Miller family who were killed in a train-auto crash in Berne on Memorial Day.

The funeral for the oldest member was on September 27, 1920, for Emanuel Liechty, father of Emil Liechty. He was ninety-five years, eight months and eleven days old.

No doubt the funeral for the next oldest member was that of Abraham Sprunger, father of Rev. S. F. Sprunger, and known to everyone as "der alt Mann." He was ninety-two years, eight months and twelve days old when he died November 1, 1889. 

Some of the earlier year books record deaths so the following records from 1904 to January 1, 1911 give the date of death, not the date of the funeral.

We are indebted to Emil Liechty for the list of funerals. 

During the years that he served as janitor there were two hundred eighty-nine church funerals, and he missed tolling the bell for only two, those of his son, Clinton, April 26, 1915, and

his father, September 27, 1920. His list begins with October 1, 1911. The figure following the name indicates the age. 

Many church members were not buried from the church and one can see at a glance that many of the funerals were for non-church members. 1904

CHRONOLOGY 

307 1838 1839 1845 1852 1853 1856 1858 1860 1865 1866 1868 1869 1871 1872 1874 1876 1877 

CHRONOLOGY 

September 15, arrival of first Mennonites in Adams 

County. (Christian and Peter Baumgartner.) 

August, arrival of Rev. David Baumgartner. 

March 18, Christian Baumgartner elected minister. 

March, arrival of Ulrich Kipfer and Matthias Strahm. 

June-July, arrival of Sprunger group and organization of Berne congregation.

July, ordination of Peter S. Lehman as elder. 

November, death of Rev. David Baumgartner. 

Resignation of Rev. Christian Baumgartner. 

April 13, Christian Sprunger elected minister.

Berne congregation began building church. 

Rev. Matthias Strahm left Mennonite church. 

Or thereabout, Baumgartner church built. 

Berne church completed. 

May 11, arrival of Rev. Peter Habegger. 

June 25, Christian Augsburger elected minister. 

March 12, death of Rev. Ulrich Kipfer. 

October 21, Christian Baumgartner elected elder. 

March 17, Christian Sprunger elected elder. 

Late March, Rev. P. S. Lehman left for Missouri. 

August 23, S. F. Sprunger elected to ministry. 

Rev. Christian Augsburger left the Mennonite Church. 

S. F. Sprunger began the ministry. 

May 15, death of Rev. Peter Habegger. 

S. F. Sprunger's congregations joined the General Conference. 

March 5, S. F. Sprunger ordained as elder by Rev. 

Christian Krehbiel of Summerfield, Illinois. 

May 3, Sunday School organized. 

Arrival of Peter M. Neuenschwander. 

About, joined Western District Conference. 

October 7, first recorded business meeting, election of deacons.

1878 1879 1880 1882 1883 1884 1886 1887 S. F. Sprunger elected delegate to Western District Conference. 

August 24, death of Rev. Christian Baumgartner.

November, Western District Conference entertained in 

Baumgartner church. 

May, first trustees elected. 

Bought 1.16 acres land of Peter Lehman for $60.

November 9, dedication of new church, on south side of road. 

November 16, Christian Sprunger's congregation met in new church for worship. 

About, Sunday evening services began. 

Beginning of library. 

January 1, first Union Communion service of S. F. 

Sprunger's congregations.

November, General Conference entertained in Berne church for first time. 

December 15, bought .4 acre land of Peter Lehman for $120.

January, J. F. Lehman elected correspondent to Bundesbote. 

January, gave up meetings in Baumgartner church. 

February 4, Temperance Society organized. 

September 5, Dan Hirschler ordained travelling preacher by S. F. Sprunger.

September 12, re-dedication of church, with C. Sprunger and S. F. Sprunger congregations united. 

October 3, all three congregations united for Communion service.

January 3, I. A. Sommer called to ministry. 

January 30, I. A. Sommer preached first sermon.

February 3, Women's Missionary Society organized. 

April 7, about 340 took Communion.

October 2, I. A. Sommer installed into the ministry by S. F. Sprunger.

Professor Rappard here and preached to large audience.

CHRONOLOGY 

309 1888 1890 1891 1892 1894 1895 1896 1897 1898 1899 1900 1901 1902 

January 2, Joel Lehman called to be evangelist. 

January 19, Joel Lehman formally installed as travelling preacher. 

September 25, congregation ordered 150 copies of Gesangbuch mit Noten. 

First rendition of "Creation."

July-August, S. F. Sprunger had leave of absence for travel in Europe.

First rendition of "The Messiah." 

January 25, death of Christian B. Lehman. 

Bought land for M. R. E. Cemetery. (William Gross was first person buried in new cemetery in October, 1892, by Amos Reusser). 

January 8, first ushers appointed.

February, Christian Endeavor Society organized. 

December 5, Frederick Hahn of East Indies spoke. 

May 31, bought .14 acre land of John Bixler for $120. 

June 29, death of church janitor, Benjamin F. Lehman. 

October 9, death of Deacon Daniel Z. Sprunger. 

November 26, first Thanksgiving sale. 

October 10, Rev. Christian Sprunger preached last sermon. 

Decided to pay yearly salary to S. F. Sprunger. 

April 8, organization of Male Choir. 

February 3, death of Rev. Peter S. Lehman.

June 18, Rev. Kröker of Bethel College preached (in Reformed church in forenoon). Missionaries Mr. 

and Mrs. Friesen of India spoke to large audience in afternoon in C. C. Sprunger's grove. 

July 30, re-dedication of church. 

January 22, John G. Paton of New Hebrides spoke. 

May 8, 9, Alexander Merensky spoke.

July 7, P. P. Bilhorn here. 

April 28, Jubilee Celebration. 

November 14, S. F. Sprunger and E. A. Luginbill signed note for new $400.00 organ. (Choral Society paid $50.00; Sunday School $50.00; Christian

1903 1904 1905 1906 1907 1909 1910 1911 

Endeavor $25.00. Besides these there were 150 donors, 14 were not called on-23 objected strongly and Sprunger and Luginbill had to pay $1.95.) 

June, resignation of S. F. Sprunger. 

October 18, death of Rev. Christian Sprunger. 

November 16, arrival of Rev. and Mrs. J. W. Kliewer. 

November 22, J. W. Kliewer preached first sermon.

December 23, funeral of Joel Welty. 

Installed electric lights. 

July 10, H. van der Smissen of Amsterdam, Holland, preached. 

August 22, S. H. Hadley of New York City here. 

January, first Union Meeting, S. D. Gordon, English speaker 

F. M. Leich, German speaker 

Charles Gabriel, song leader. 

July 3, Missionary H. L. Weiss, Chili, South America, here.

October 17, death of Church Secretary C. M. Stauffer. 

September 24, death of Church Treasurer A. A. Sprunger. 

June 30, first observance of Children's Day. 

November 25, death of Church Treasurer Daniel Sprunger. 

April 13, decided to build new church. 

July 10, cornerstone laid. 

July 15, Rev. J. W. Kliewer resigned, then reconsidered. 

December 22, bought .41 acre land of Mrs. Maria Bixler for $670. 

February 19-March 15, no church services due to small pox epidemic. 

April 30, Rev. J. W. Kliewer resigned. 

August 3, Rev. J. W. Kliewer left Berne for Bethel 

College, Newton, Kansas. 

S. F. Sprunger resumed pastorate.

CHRONOLOGY 

311 1912 1913 1914 1915 1917 1918 1919 

December 10, 17, 24, no church services due to small pox epidemic. 

March 31, last Sunday services held in old church. 

April 5, Good Friday, special farewell services in old church. 

April 7, Easter Sunday, dedication of new church. 

May 12, first observance of Mother's Day in the church. 

June 8, arrival of Rev. and Mrs. P. R. Schroeder. 

June 9, Rev. Schroeder preached first sermon. 

August 5, decided to establish nursery, also to change 

Sunday School hour to forenoon.

December, organization of Ladies' Choir. 

August 19-20 first conference of all Mennonites. (About 

200 guests from 8 sects present).

Intermediate Christian Endeavor Society organized. 

January 25, Rev. P. R. Schroeder ordained as elder by

S. F. Sprunger. 

March 1, dedication of pipe organ. 

March 22, individual Communion cups first used. 

April 10, death of Deacon Frederick Sprunger. 

June 10, death of Sam Lehman. 

English services held each second Sunday evening of the month. 

September 19-26, no services due to diphtheria epidemic. 

Old organ sent to Aberdeen, Idaho, Mennonite church of which Rev. E. J. Neuenschwander was pastor. 

Boys were drafted to serve in World War. 

English services each Sunday evening. 

January 13, no services held due to bad storm and excessively cold weather. 

From 3rd Sunday in October to 4th Sunday in December no church services because of flu epidemic. 

Frieda N. Sprunger, (Mrs. P. J. Boehr) served the church as deaconess for 6 months. 

September 14, S. F. Sprunger preached last sermon (on John 10:28).

1920 1921 1922 1923 1924 1925 1927 1928 1929 1930 1931 1932 1933 1934 1935 

February 9, decided to build parsonage. 

February 26, last meeting held in old church, lecture by Samuel Grathwell. 

October 19, P. R. Schroeder family moved into new parsonage.

June, Summer Bible School organized. 

Acousticon installed.

Decided that English services should be held at least one Sunday each month in the morning. 

November 16 dates the passing away of Rev. S. F.

Sprunger, the pastor who for more than a third of a century served not only the Mennonites of Berne. but also Mennonites far from his home church. 

March 19, death of I. A. Sommer. 

Church closed from August 3 on for 7 weeks for redecorating the church. Services were held in 

Reformed church, Lehman's grove and Berne Auditorium.

Mid-Week Committee organized. 

May 1, decided to hold one missionary service and to take a collection for missions once a month. 

January 28, death of Deacon Hiram Baumgartner. 

July 29, farewell for Rev. P. R. Schroeder and family. 

August 1, Rev. P. R. Schroeder and family left for 

Freeman, South Dakota. 

September 7, arrival of Rev. C. H. Suckau and family. 

September 11, Rev. Suckau preached first sermon. 

July 15, decided to discontinue Thanksgiving sales. 

August 27-28, All-Mennonite Conference convened here.

Decided to level off and stone old cemetery for parking lot. 

August 14, farewell services for C. H. A. van der 

Smissen family.

Church barn torn down and parking lot made. 

March, Young Men's Chorus organized. 

February, Young Ladies' Chorus organized.

PRESENT CHURCH AND AUXILIARY OFFICERS 

313 1936 1937 1938 Began remodeling basement for Sunday School department. 

January, German services changed to one Sunday afternoon each month. 

February 9, Dr. J. W. Kliewer passed away. 

July-August, Rev. C. H. Suckau on leave of absence to Europe.

July-August, Rev. Peter J. Boehr substitute pastor. 

September 18-25, Centennial Celebration.
